The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #006980;">Gene P</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #006980;"><i>pebble</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006980;">Gene P</span> produces colonies with a rough, uneven surface that resembles a collection of tiny pebbles.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #9d2a62;">Gene T</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #9d2a62;"><i>toxic</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9d2a62;">Gene T</span> secretes a toxic compound that inhibits or kills other microbial colonies nearby.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #426c09;">Gene H</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #426c09;"><i>hairy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #426c09;">Gene H</span> cells develop long, thread-like filaments that extend outward, creating a hairy, shaggy texture on the colony.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006596;">Gene X</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #006596;"><i>xenon</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006596;">Gene X</span> cells emit a faint glow under UV light, as if they were fluorescent.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse;
